Four notes. Pilot sizes and costs in the table are TARGETs and estimates taken from the cited sections. Kernel
reflection is tiered [F] in §12.4 and is not counted against the cap; if KILL-REFLECT fires it moves to C.1.3.
Payload slosh by an equivalent mechanical model, robot-advected films and articulated cord and rug proxies are [F]
(§19.10.3 to §19.10.5) and are likewise outside the cap; resolved free-surface fluid, cloth and rods exist only
inside item 2. For items 9, 10 and 11 the [M] part is funded only after the [F] part of the same section has
passed its own gate.

### C.2 The killed-idea register

Each entry gives the strong form as it was proposed, the refutation with its numbers, and what survives with the
section, gate and kill gate that hold it. An entry that corresponds to a hypothesis of §0.8 carries its identifier
and state. The reversal condition of an entry is the measurement named in its "survives" line: a verdict changes
only on new evidence of the kind that produced it. Entries are numbered through the whole register and are cited
as "C.2 entry n". Shortcuts that are wrong for a hand-checkable reason, as distinct from mechanisms that were
proposed and then deflated, are in Appendix N.

#### C.2.1 Arithmetic, precision and kernels

**1. Half precision on tensor cores for the contact solve** (H-15, REFUTED)
- *Strong form.* Factor the contact Hessian H = M + JᵀDJ in half precision on matrix accelerators, refine in
  single precision, and gain a factor of four or more on the solve.
- *Refutation.* DESIGN arithmetic: half precision has unit round-off u₁₆ = 2⁻¹¹ ≈ 4.9 × 10⁻⁴, and refinement
  contracts only if κ·u₁₆ < 1. A rank-one contact operator with the proposed shift ρ = 10⁻²·tr(W)/48 has
  κ = 1 + 48/0.01 = 4,801, so κ·u₁₆ = 2.34. FACT (software emulation on three reference models): after
  unit-diagonal scaling, κ·u₁₆ ≥ 1 in 37 to 64% of sampled contact states and the refined solve fails outright in
  9 to 18%. Cost: k refinement steps at about 3n² operations against a factor of n³/3 is a ratio of 9k/n, which is
  2.3 at n = 27 and k = 7, 1.5 at n = 35 and k = 6, and 3.0 at n = 18 and k = 6. Refinement alone costs more than
  the factorization it was meant to cheapen. FACT: the published four- to five-fold gain is against double
  precision at 22,000 unknowns, and against a single-precision baseline the theory promises at most two.
- *Survives.* Nothing about contact solves. Single precision in power-of-two scaled variables (§18.12, §35.10;
  BET-08). A request for a half-precision solve or state is refused (`FROB-REF-245`).

**2. Tensor-core products for a 256-wide policy**
- *Strong form.* Run policy inference and the learner's matrix products on matrix accelerators at reduced
  precision.
- *Refutation.* FACT: the learner and inference together are about 13% of training wall-clock on one incumbent
  stack, so removing them entirely gives at most 1/(1 − 0.13) = 1.15; and reduced precision leaves determinism
  class D2.
- *Survives.* Plain `f32` products in the kernel dialect. Device matrix paths only inside the labeled D4 training
  profile, whose results are re-evaluated on a stricter profile (§35.2, §37.11).

**3. Supercompiled per-design kernels as a throughput lever** (H-04, REFUTED for recursion-only specialization)
- *Strong form.* Partial evaluation of the recursions on one fixed design, with equality saturation, gives at
  least 1.5 times the throughput of the universal kernel at no more than 60 s of compilation per design.
- *Refutation.* FACT (an incumbent's event trace, humanoid, 8,192 worlds): of 262 ns per world-step, the stages a
  design-specialized recursion could touch sum to 53 ns, 20%; the solve is 124 ns and constraint construction
  38 ns. DESIGN arithmetic: an infinitely fast recursion gives 262/209 = 1.25 and one twice as fast gives 1.11; a
  manipulation scene has a share of 16% and a ceiling of 1.19; a ceiling of 1.5 needs a share of one third. FACT:
  the incumbent's cold kernel compilation takes about 96 s.
- *Survives.* Specialization for tournament survivors only, and only if our own M2 profile shows a specializable
  share of at least one third (§21.18; GATE-SPARK-14; KILL-SPECIALIZE). Derivation stays for correctness,
  certificates and the derivative kernels of CPU solvers (C-14). Topology is data, so a new design recompiles
  nothing (§21.11). Structure-exploiting constraint assembly and solve is a separate and open question (H-04b,
  ACTIVE): its sparsity is carried as data, it is measured at M2, and it is not counted in the thesis until then
  (§16.23).

**4. Equality saturation and superoptimization of kernel arithmetic**
- *Strong form.* An e-graph with a solver over real-arithmetic rewrites, or a stochastic superoptimizer, extracts
  faster kernel bodies than common-subexpression elimination.
- *Refutation.* Every accepted rewrite changes bits, so each needs an error bound, a battery run and a new kernel
  identity. No sound equivalence check exists for floating-point code. On the trace of entry 3 the solve alone is
  124 of 262 ns, 47% of the step, which rewrites of the recursions do not touch.
- *Survives.* Common-subexpression elimination, and rule-trace rewrite certificates in the exact class E and the
  bounded class R (§21.7; DC-11). The method is reopened only on the profile condition of DC-11.

**5. Fast-math switches in the kernel pipeline**
- *Strong form.* Contraction of multiply-adds, an approximate arithmetic mode and built-in sine and cosine in
  batched kernels, beside a three-way parity gate.
- *Refutation.* Each switch lets a compiler or a device choose the rounding, so the CPU, CUDA and Metal kernels of
  one source no longer agree bit for bit and the parity gates (GATE-SPARK-01, GATE-SPARK-02) cannot pass. The two
  requirements exclude each other.
- *Survives.* The bans of §35.6 (ADR-SPARK-02): no fast-math, no fused multiply-add, no built-in transcendentals
  in batched kernels, no floating-point atomics. An explicit, declared fused multiply-add is OPEN-NUM-1.

**6. Unordered floating-point scatter, accepted if 99.9% of windows replay bitwise**
- *Strong form.* Drop the ordered scatter for unordered atomics if the two agree on 99.9% of windows.
- *Refutation.* DESIGN arithmetic: 0.999¹⁰⁰⁰ = 0.37. A campaign of a thousand windows would replay exactly about
  one time in three, and a replay that is bitwise most of the time is not bitwise.
- *Survives.* A stable-key sort and a segmented reduction (`K-seg-reduce`; §35.7). Integer atomics only for
  maximum, minimum, or and wrapping add.

**7. A derived launch-cost advantage, and a megakernel at high occupancy**
- *Strong form.* An 8- to 20-fold launch-cost advantage from fewer graph nodes; one fused megakernel at 75%
  occupancy or more, with a lossless 27-float summary of each limb.
- *Refutation.* FACT: a warm launch-graph replay has near-constant host cost, so cost per node does not
  accumulate. DESIGN arithmetic on the proposals' own figures: 2 warps of 48 per multiprocessor is 4.2% occupancy,
  and 12 of 48 is 25%, not 75%. The limb summary is exact only without base-coupled contact, and its Schur update
  is only positive semidefinite.
- *Survives.* The latency from an edit to the first GPU step, as a measured number (§17.8). Limb port packets as
  an intermediate tier (KILL-SKEL-PORT). The fused per-world step kernel as an [M] pilot whose benefit is unproven
  in sign (§21.18; KILL-MEGA).

**8. Staged erasure of algebra as a source of runtime speed**
- *Strong form.* Erasing algebraic structure at compile time makes the runtime strictly faster than a
  conventional engine.
- *Refutation.* It restates the plan's own compile-time position and names no new mechanism. Its figure, 1.875 in
  floating-point operations, is counted on a step that is bound by memory traffic (§36.11) and not by arithmetic.
- *Survives.* A compiler rule: proofs, tags and types are erased before runtime (§21.7).

**9. Per-world adaptive time tiles and time-parallel integration**
- *Strong form.* Advance each world through tiles of up to 64 steps between interactions, or integrate in
  parallel in time with coarse and fine propagators.
- *Refutation.* Separate worlds are already independent. DESIGN arithmetic: policy and sensor frames at 50 to
  100 Hz are observation fences, and at physics steps of 1 to 2 ms they bound a tile to 5 to 20 steps. Contact
  makes coarse and fine propagators disagree, and tiles make shapes dynamic.
- *Survives.* Fixed decimation in a static launch graph (§21.11).

#### C.2.2 Memory, forks, telemetry and scheduling

**10. Copy-on-write forks on device**
- *Strong form.* Fork device world state by copy-on-write, so that a fork costs nothing until it diverges.
- *Refutation.* State is write-dense: every step rewrites every world's configuration, velocity, contacts and
  warm starts, so a fork has copied everything after one step and pays an indirection on every read. DESIGN
  arithmetic: the whole copy is small. 8,192 worlds × 30 kB = 0.25 GB, at most about a millisecond at the memory
  bandwidth of an RTX 4090, and so at most a quarter of one 4.1 ms batch step at 2.0 M world-steps/s.
- *Survives.* Fork by indexed copy of the chosen rows into free slots (`K-snapshot`), with warm template
  environments kept resident (§27.3, §29.8).

**11. Order-dependent quantile sketches**
- *Strong form.* Mergeable quantile sketches for telemetry quantiles across shards and devices.
- *Refutation.* Hand-checkable: a two-centroid sketch that merges its closest pair, fed {0, 3, 5, 9}. In the
  order 0, 3, 5, 9 it ends as (8/3 with weight 3; 9 with weight 1). In the order 0, 9, 5, 3 it ends as (1.5 with
  weight 2; 7 with weight 2). The same data give different sketches, so the statistic depends on shard count and
  arrival order, which the batch-composition invariance of §35.5 forbids.
- *Survives.* Integer log-bin histograms (`K-x-hist`): bins from the bit pattern, integer counts, merge by
  addition (§29.7, §35.7).

**12. Minimal perfect hashing or succinct structures for brick directories**
- *Strong form.* Compress the directory of scene bricks with a minimal perfect hash or a succinct structure.
- *Refutation.* DESIGN arithmetic: a dense directory of 8³-voxel bricks with 4-byte slots is 63 × 63 × 19 = 75,411
  slots, 0.3 MB, for a 10 × 10 × 3 m interior at 2 cm; 587,500 slots, 2.3 MB, for a 60 × 40 × 8 m warehouse at
  4 cm; and 18.8 MB for that warehouse at 2 cm. That is about 0.4% of the dense 16-bit field it indexes, and a
  hash adds a second, verifying read to every lookup.
- *Survives.* A dense directory per scene variant over a shared brick pool (§19.15; KILL-POOL).

**13. Live zero-copy reads of device state by the CPU**
- *Strong form.* On unified memory the CPU reads live GPU state in under a microsecond with no copy.
- *Refutation.* FACT (vendor documentation): shared storage must be quiescent before the other processor reads
  it, and device writes are visible only when a command buffer completes. DESIGN arithmetic: the data are tiny. A
  humanoid's integration state is about 540 bytes, so shadowing 256 worlds at 50 Hz moves 6.9 MB/s.
- *Survives.* Fenced snapshots on any backend (rule R1 of §21.9; `K-snapshot`), which also serve the two-card
  machine by explicit read-back.

**14. An always-on referee at zero copy cost** (H-11, NARROWED)
- *Strong form.* Idle CPU cores shadow GPU worlds with the referee at zero copy cost and catch tier-sensitive
  behaviour during training at no more than 5% of throughput.
- *Refutation.* Coverage is the ratio of referee rate to device rate: about 0.1 to 2.4% of GPU world-seconds on
  any backend if the referee runs at 0.2 to 1.0 times real time per core on 24 cores, and more if it is faster
  (HYPOTHESIS, §0.8; measured at M2); unified memory is not the lever (entry 13). DESIGN arithmetic:
  detecting a five-point gap in success rate needs n ≈ 16·p·(1 − p)/δ² episodes per tier, 576 at p = 0.9 and
  1,600 at p = 0.5. The mechanism adds cost and removes no training or search.
- *Survives.* A sampled background referee with a reported coverage figure, as a correctness guard (§16.11;
  GATE-FID-07 at coverage of at least 0.1% and a throughput loss of at most 5%; KILL-SHADOW; BET-34).

**15. Feedback control of solver effort on wall-clock**
- *Strong form.* A proportional-integral-derivative or model-predictive controller reads measured step time and
  adjusts solver iterations and effort profiles.
- *Refutation.* Wall-clock feedback into iteration counts makes a result depend on machine load. The same world
  address run on a loaded and on an idle machine executes different iteration counts and yields different
  digests, so replay under classes D1 and D2 breaks and paired statistics are refused. No calculation is needed:
  one such pair is the counterexample.
- *Survives.* Effort profiles are model identities, chosen by a guard table with hysteresis that reads solver
  residuals and never time (DC-08; §28.19). The lockstep iteration count is a constant of the shape class
  (§16.6).

**16. Causal tile reuse across replays**
- *Strong form.* Reuse the unchanged tiles of a replay, for a factor near 12.7 if 95% of the replay is reusable.
- *Refutation.* The robot's neighbourhood dominates the compute and is always dirty. The 95% premise holds for
  far-field state that costs almost nothing to recompute.
- *Survives.* Sharing of static far-field fields through the brick pool (§19.15).

#### C.2.3 Worlds and physics

**17. Full-room fluid, cloth or granular matter in every training world** (H-16, REFUTED as stated)
- *Strong form.* Fluid films, cables and cloth coupled to rigid robots inside training worlds, at 10% or more of
  rigid-only throughput when active in 5% of worlds.
- *Refutation.* DESIGN arithmetic: a D3Q19 lattice in single precision with two population buffers needs
  19 × 4 × 2 = 152 bytes per cell. A 10 × 10 × 3 m room at 1 cm is 3.0 × 10⁸ cells, 45.6 GB for one world and
  about 374 TB at 8,192 worlds; at 5 mm one world needs 365 GB, above the working-set limit of the large-memory
  machine. A spill film of 0.1 to 1 mm needs cells of 0.25 mm: 2.56 × 10⁸ cells, 39 GB per square metre of floor.
  Throughput: 0.95 + 0.05·c ≤ 10 gives c ≤ 181 rigid worlds per coupled world, and the sibling cloth solver takes
  tens of CPU seconds per drape (FACT).
- *Survives.* A contact-level film-friction law and rigid proxies [S] (§18.8, §19.10.2); whether they reproduce
  the failures of coupled models is untested and is decided by the pilot of §19.10.7. Advected films, slosh and
  articulated proxies [F]. Coupled islands [M] on the CPU referee for finalists only (§19.10.6; KILL-LIVING;
  BET-36).

**18. Lattice fluid at control rate**
- *Strong form.* Advance fluid tiles once per 50 Hz control tick inside the training batch.
- *Refutation.* DESIGN arithmetic: the lattice Mach number is Ma = √3·u·Δt/Δx. At u = 1 m/s and Δt = 20 ms it is
  0.35 at 10 cm cells, 3.5 at 1 cm and 6.9 at 5 mm, against a low-Mach limit of 0.3 (FACT: `fs-lbm`). Holding
  Ma = 0.1 at 5 mm needs Δt ≤ 0.29 ms, 70 fluid steps per control tick. The relaxation time is
  τ = 3·ν·Δt/Δx² + ½; for water at 10 cm cells and 20 ms steps τ − ½ = 6 × 10⁻⁶, where a single-relaxation scheme
  is not stable.
- *Survives.* Payload slosh as one to three sprung masses per axis [F] (§19.10.4; KILL-SLOSH). A 2.5-dimensional
  advected film field [F] (§19.10.3; KILL-ADVECT). A lattice fluid only inside an [M] island, as an offline oracle.

**19. Symplectic claims beyond a free rigid body**
- *Strong form.* The variational integrator is symplectic for the articulated robot, and its energy figures
  carry over.
- *Refutation.* The four steps of the trapezoidal scheme are derived for one rigid body. On an articulated tree
  the mass matrix depends on configuration and a kick–drift splitting is no longer the variational step.
  Dissipative contact impulses end energy conservation by design. The published energy figures are for one body.
- *Survives.* C-36 [F] in the referee and the `strict` profile, with the free body and the heavy top as oracles
  and no symplectic claim for a tree (§16.7; KILL-VARIATIONAL). Energy is audited by the ledger, not assumed
  (§16.10, §29.9).

**20. Refusing simultaneous multi-foot impacts**
- *Strong form.* Treat simultaneous impacts as ill-posed and refuse them.
- *Refutation.* Trot, bound and pronk put two or four feet down at one instant by symmetry. A refusal removes the
  gaits that the gait analysis exists to find.
- *Survives.* One coupled impulse solve per island, never an imposed order (§16.5). For orbit derivatives a
  simultaneous group is split by ±δ and the one-sided Jacobians are compared (§22.9.4; ADR-REFLEX-05).

**21. Curvature as a roller-misalignment test**
- *Strong form.* Spanning curvature of the mechanical connection certifies, at preflight, that an
  omnidirectional base is correctly assembled.
- *Refutation.* Hand-checkable: a mecanum base assembled with all four roller axes parallel. The map from body
  twist to wheel rates drops from rank 3 to rank 2, and the growth vector is (2, 3) where an omnidirectional base
  has (3). The missing direction is still reached by a bracket, exactly as on a differential drive, so the
  curvature test passes the defective base.
- *Survives.* The growth vector of A-07 at preflight stage 4 for wheeled and tracked bases. Curvature (A-09,
  C-08) only for shape-changing kinematic locomotors (§14.11, §28.4).

**22. A global lifted linear model for contact-rich predictive control**
- *Strong form.* One Koopman-style linear model in a lifted space replaces the simulator inside predictive
  control.
- *Refutation.* No finite invariant subspace survives contact switching. DESIGN arithmetic: a quadratic lift of
  60 states has C(62, 2) = 1,891 monomials, and a linear operator on them has 1,891² ≈ 3.58 × 10⁶ coefficients to
  identify.
- *Survives.* Parked (C.1.3). At most one bounded experiment on a single soil regime.

**23. Gradients through everything**
- *Strong form.* Differentiate scenes and whole rollouts end to end and search by gradient, for a factor of 150.
- *Refutation.* The gradient of a binary failure outcome is zero almost everywhere. The proposal's own step
  counts give a factor of 2.54, not 150. Incumbents already differentiate, with bias through contact.
- *Survives.* Derivatives with validity labels on regular segments and return maps (C-16). Paired finite
  differences over short horizons (§17.13; KILL-FDGRAD). Gradients through contact are deferred to M6 (D-8).

**24. Runtime composition wrappers around the step**
- *Strong form.* Compose robot modules at run time through higher-categorical wrappers.
- *Refutation.* Contacts on different limbs couple through the base in one contact operator, so a simultaneous
  multi-foot solve does not factor along module boundaries. A run-time wrapper also breaks the fixed data layout
  that batched kernels need.
- *Survives.* Composition over typed ports, lowered at compile time and kept to one data structure and five
  functors (C-12; §14.6, §14.9).

**25. The entropy balance as printed, with entropy as the state of a lumped thermal node**
- *Strong form.* Carry entropy as the state of each thermal node and add a separate irreversible production
  term.
- *Refutation.* The terms already present are the irreversible production, so the added term counts it twice.
- *Survives.* One thermal node with temperature as its state. Stored heat is a store and not a loss on the
  ledger (C-38; §15.5, §15.11).

#### C.2.4 Control, learning and transfer

**26. Sampling control as a stand-in evaluator for a trained policy** (H-01, NARROWED)
- *Strong form.* Sampling-based predictive control, with the batched simulator as its model, completes tasks for
  at least 80% of well-formed designs with no training and serves as the acceptance evaluator.
- *Refutation.* DESIGN arithmetic on the smallest published setting (30 samples, 3 iterations, a 0.9 s horizon
  at 2 ms, 50 Hz): one simulated second costs 30 × 3 × 450 × 50 = 2.0 M world-steps against 500 for a policy, a
  factor of 4 × 10³. A battery of 256 twenty-second episodes takes about 86 minutes of a device at 2.0 M
  world-steps/s, against 1.3 s. As acceptance evidence, bounding a failure rate at 1% with 95% confidence needs
  299 clean episodes: about 99 GPU-minutes per quadruped design at 0.33 GPU-minute each, against under 10 for
  training once.
- *Survives.* A screening rung of three episodes per design by default (a paired screening comparison may use up
  to the break-even count, about 30 for the quadruped setting and 60 for the humanoid at a 20 ms planning step), an
  out-of-distribution fallback and a source of demonstrations (§22.8; GATE-REFLEX-06; KILL-MPC; BET-27). The cost is
  about 0.33 and 1 GPU-minutes per 20 s episode at a 20 ms planning step and about 1.7 and 5.2 at 4 ms. A request
  to cite it as acceptance evidence for a trained policy is refused (`FROB-REF-817`); where it is the delivered
  controller, its own episodes are admitted under a frozen planner record.

**27. One universal policy** (H-02, NARROWED)
- *Strong form.* One pretrained, design-conditioned policy walks any unseen design with no adaptation at 70% of
  specialist return, and reaches specialist return in 10% of from-scratch steps.
- *Refutation.* FACT (published results): across topology-diverse designs, held-out robots score 1,489.6 against
  4,030.6 on familiar ones, 37%; one test morphology scores 136.82 with no adaptation against 1,406.58 from
  scratch, about 10%; fine-tuning gains two to three times. The results that reach 85 to 97% used about 5 days on
  160 GPUs, 19,200 GPU-hours, which is 400 days on two RTX 4090.
- *Survives.* Family policies per morphology class, on flat or mildly varied ground with fixed actuation: 70 to
  85% of specialist return with no adaptation, and fine-tuning at about 2 to 25% of a from-scratch run stopped at
  matched return. On interior terrain within a class there is no published figure. Out-of-class designs are
  declined (§23.11; GATE-CORTEX-11; KILL-FAMILY; BET-15).

**28. Policy inheritance for any edit** (H-21, NARROWED)
- *Strong form.* A child design reaches specialist return from its parent's policy in at most 20% of
  from-scratch steps for parameter edits and 50% for single-limb topology edits, along chains of any length.
- *Refutation.* FACT (published transfer results): single-limb topology edits cost 33 to 100% of from-scratch
  steps and show no gain on flat terrain, and chained inheritance loses plasticity.
- *Survives.* Inheritance for parameter edits, as the default once it has beaten an early-stopped from-scratch
  control for the edit class. Transfer from a class prior costs about 2 to 25% of such a run; the one published
  parent-to-child figure is about 50%; no source measures a small typed parameter edit. Resets follow a measured
  regression of an audit twin, with a depth schedule as fallback (§23.12; GATE-CORTEX-13; KILL-INHERIT; BET-16).

**29. A geometric-algebra tree policy as a source of sample efficiency** (H-24, NARROWED)
- *Strong form.* Link tokens with multivector features and message passing on the kinematic tree beat a plain
  morphology transformer by at least 1.3 in sample efficiency at equal parameters.
- *Refutation.* FACT: geometric-algebra layers ran 1.4 to 7.5 times slower per epoch in published work, so equal
  parameters is not equal compute. No locomotion evidence exists, and the expected gain is 0 to 1.3. A factor of
  1.3 on an amortized pretraining run is not a source of the ten-fold.
- *Survives.* Invariance to heading and to description conventions through canonical inputs and augmentation
  (C-21; §23.11). The tree policy as an [M] three-arm experiment with the plain transformer as control (§23.14;
  KILL-GATREE).

**30. Gait continuation at one percent of a training run** (H-05, NARROWED)
- *Strong form.* Continuation of a hybrid periodic orbit carries a stable gait across design changes at no more
  than 1% of a training run per design point, and yields a controller.
- *Refutation.* FACT: published full-order orbit solves take about 4 to 260 s of CPU at 20 to 68 states. DESIGN
  arithmetic: that is 2% to 108% of a four-minute training run, and 1% to 63% of the 410 s figure. The result is
  a nominal orbit on regular ground, not a controller the referee accepts.
- *Survives.* A gait-orbit analysis with continuation in design parameters, labeled screening (§22.9;
  GATE-GAIT-03; KILL-GAIT; BET-26).

**31. Proof-guided control for full-order legged robots** (H-20, NARROWED)
- *Strong form.* Sum-of-squares funnels, hybrid-zero-dynamics gaits and barrier shields with checked
  certificates yield deployable safety filters for real designs.
- *Refutation.* FACT (sibling code): the semidefinite solver costs O(N⁴) for an N × N Gram block, and its
  largest region-of-attraction example has two states. Certificates exist for templates of about 4 to 12 states,
  and a template proof is not a fact about a robot.
- *Survives.* An energy-budget tier and reduced-order barrier filters whose margin is measured statistically,
  class Tested (§22.11, §22.12; KILL-ENERGY, KILL-SHIELD; BET-25). The word "deployable" is not used, and every
  export carries the no-authorization flags.

**32. The clairvoyant barrier condition, and two closed forms for shields**
- *Strong form.* (a) Robust invariance from "for every disturbance w there is an input u". (b) The smallest
  friction cone suffices for every force. (c) A closed-form projection for a single-row filter.
- *Refutation.* (a) If safety needs u = d with d ∈ {−1, +1}, each pointwise problem is feasible and no one input
  serves both. (b) An infimum over a smaller set is larger, so the cone at μ_min is optimistic for forces the
  environment applies. (c) With u_π = (1, 0), the box [0, 1]² and the row u₁ + u₂ ≥ 1.5, the formula returns
  (1.25, 0.25), outside the box; clipping gives (1, 0.25), which violates the row; the optimum is (1, 0.5).
- *Survives.* The order ∃u ∀(w, e) over the belief set (C-31). Split cones: the inner cone at μ_min for forces
  the robot commands, the outer cone for forces the environment may apply. The breakpoint method for one row, and
  projected Newton with a KKT check after the fact otherwise (§22.12; GATE-SHIELD-02).

#### C.2.5 Search, statistics and decision rules

**33. Exact reuse after a geometry edit** (H-09, REFUTED for exact reuse)
- *Strong form.* Dependency tracking lets a typical single-parameter edit reuse at least 70% of prior evaluation
  work.
- *Refutation.* At least 90% of per-design compute is rollouts and training (FACT: training takes 4 to 20
  minutes per design; a cold kernel compile is about 96 s once per machine and under 1 s cached). The mass
  matrix, bias forces and contact Jacobians enter every step, so no rollout is reusable bit for bit after a mass
  or geometry edit. Exact reuse weighted by compute is at most about 10%, below the hypothesis's own falsifier
  of 30%.
- *Survives.* A memo graph for analyses, step plans, kernels and scene compiles (§28.14). Dependency-directed
  revalidation, which says which verdicts survive an edit (§28.13; `result.revalidate`; GATE-ATEL-11;
  KILL-REUSE; BET-33). Training avoided by a warm start is a different quantity: it belongs to inheritance (H-21)
  and is counted once, in its own column of the reuse ledger (§28.26).

**34. Verdict reuse by sensitivity margin**
- *Strong form.* Inherit a verdict across an edit when a sensitivity estimate says the metric moved less than the
  decision margin, for savings beyond a cache.
- *Refutation.* FACT (executed pilot): a correctly keyed dependency graph matched the semantic rule saving for
  saving, three pair evaluations and two reductions each, and omitting one dependency returned a stale promotion.
  No certificate bounds how an edit moves a rollout statistic through contact.
- *Survives.* Reuse keyed by dependency, with polarity where a proof rule supplies it (C-18; §25.8). One false
  reuse removes the rule (KILL-AUGUR-REUSE).

**35. Joint paving of high-dimensional design boxes** (H-06, NARROWED)
- *Strong form.* Interval and Taylor-model evaluation decides at least half the volume of a 10- to
  20-dimensional design box without simulation, within 10⁴ boxes.
- *Refutation.* DESIGN arithmetic: 10⁴ boxes are log₂ 10⁴ = 13.3 bisections, less than one cut per axis in 20
  dimensions. Uniform boxes keep a side of 2^(−13.3/20) = 0.63 of the domain, and 0.40 in 10 dimensions, so a
  constraint surface that depends on most parameters crosses nearly every box. FACT: a published
  three-dimensional workspace paving ran about five hours and left an error bound of up to a third of the
  certified volume.
- *Survives.* Certificates per constraint, with bisection confined to a split set of about six parameters and the
  rest enclosed at full range, combined by products, monotonicity and witnesses (C-32; §25.6, §25.34;
  KILL-AUGUR-REGION; BET-20).

**36. A rare-event engine with bounds from splitting** (H-07, NARROWED)
- *Strong form.* Multilevel splitting or adaptive importance sampling estimates a failure probability of 10⁻⁵
  with a hundredth of the episodes and valid confidence bounds.
- *Refutation.* DESIGN arithmetic: a zero-failure 95% bound at 10⁻⁵ needs ln(20)/10⁻⁵ ≈ 3.0 × 10⁵ nominal
  episodes. FACT: the measured gains in the sources read are 2 to 20 times in one driving study and about 5 times in
  mean-square error on one policy-in-the-loop task; 10 to 100 times at 10⁻⁴ to 10⁻⁵ is a TARGET. A splitting
  estimate with adaptive levels, though unbiased, carries no finite-sample confidence bound.
- *Survives.* Crude sampling certifies absence. Bounded-weight importance sampling refutes a target and tightens an
  estimate once failures are observed, and never certifies absence more cheaply than crude sampling. Splitting and
  tilting explore (C-27; §26.9; KILL-RARE; BET-32).

**37. Extreme-value extrapolation, and a defensive mixture as the certified bound**
- *Strong form.* Certify a 10⁻⁶ tail by fitting an extreme-value law, or by importance sampling from a defensive
  mixture.
- *Refutation.* DESIGN arithmetic: checking a fitted tail at 10⁻⁶ takes the same ln(20)/10⁻⁶ ≈ 3 × 10⁶ nominal
  episodes that certify directly. A defensive mixture with nominal share β has a floor of ln(1/δ)/(β·n), never
  below crude sampling on that share.
- *Survives.* Crude and bounded-weight bounds. The defensive mixture as a lower bound for refutation only
  (DC-04).

**38. A single top cause from forks taken just before the failure** (H-08, NARROWED)
- *Strong form.* Restoring a snapshot just before a failure and applying single-factor interventions, design
  parameters included, names the cause with at least 90% accuracy.
- *Refutation.* Contact dynamics amplify small differences (FACT: a published perturbation of 5 × 10⁻⁴ degree
  diverges within 50 steps), so a top factor can change under a perturbation at round-off scale. A design
  parameter changed a moment before failure leaves the history unchanged (winding temperature, the policy's own
  training), which is a different question from whether that design would have failed. A coalition value can be
  negative and cannot establish necessity.
- *Survives.* Interventional contrasts with a flip rate and a placebo fork. Design-parameter interventions re-run
  from the episode start. Double precision is the default for one failure; single precision on the originating
  tier is admitted by a measured rule for fork windows up to about 1 s (§27.5 to §27.9, §27.19; GATE-AUTOPSY-03;
  KILL-AUTOPSY; BET-23).

**39. Value of information as a selection lever** (H-10, NARROWED)
- *Strong form.* Choosing each experiment by expected information gain cuts the simulations to a decision by at
  least three, against experiments an agent chooses.
- *Refutation.* FACT (best-arm identification results): stopping and elimination save 2 to 5 when candidates are
  separated and about nothing otherwise. Selection by information gain against a capable agent is unsupported
  and plausibly below 1.5.
- *Survives.* Value of information as a stop rule and a budget allocator at the finalist stage, and for choosing
  measurements (C-39; §28.16; GATE-ATEL-15; KILL-VOI).

**40. Rank fidelity assumed between rungs** (H-22a and H-22b, both ACTIVE and unmeasured)
- *Strong form.* A shared policy on the fast tier ranks designs as trained specialists on the referee do, at
  Kendall τ ≥ 0.7 near the top, so under one design in twenty needs training; and paired evaluation needs at most
  a fifth of the expensive samples.
- *Refutation.* FACT: no published rank agreement of this kind was found; the nearest figures are rank
  correlations of 0.40 overall and 0.4 to 0.8. DESIGN arithmetic: a control variate saves 1/(1 − ρ²), which is
  2.0 at ρ = 0.7, 5.3 at 0.9 and 10.3 at 0.95, so a fifth of the samples needs ρ ≥ 0.89.
- *Survives.* The rank-agreement experiment decides, and it reports containment and regret beside τ. A cheap rung
  selects at τ ≥ 0.7 with the winner check passed, discards only between 0.5 and 0.7, and may do neither below 0.5
  or with no record (C-26; §16.9, §23.28, §28.10; GATE-ATEL-07; KILL-RANK; BET-17).

**41. A family per dispatch, with long-horizon finite-difference gradients** (H-23, NARROWED)
- *Strong form.* Evaluating a neighbourhood of designs in one batch under a shared policy cuts agent rounds by at
  least five, and paired finite differences give usable design gradients over whole episodes.
- *Refutation.* FACT: measured compute gains of published population methods are about 3 to 4. Paired
  differences lose their pairing once trajectories diverge through contact (entry 38), so the gradient is
  unproven beyond short horizons and smooth metrics.
- *Survives.* Families per dispatch, and short-horizon paired differences as a sensitivity report (§17.12,
  §17.13; KILL-FDGRAD).

**42. Topology as data, as a capability claim** (H-03, NARROWED)
- *Strong form.* One compiled kernel simulates different trees in one batch at no more than twice the per-world
  cost of a specialized kernel, as a capability no incumbent has.
- *Refutation.* FACT: one incumbent solver publishes a mixed batch of three body plans at 0.86 of the harmonic mean
  of its homogeneous rates (about 86 thousand samples per second against about 100 thousand). DESIGN arithmetic:
  one batch padded to 64 degrees of freedom costs an 18-degree quadruped 64/18 = 3.6 on linear stages, 12.6 on
  quadratic ones and 45 on a dense cubic factor; and 256 designs × 2,048 rollouts at about 60 kB per padded
  world is 31 GB, above a 24 GB card.
- *Survives.* Per-world model variants inside padded capacities and size buckets, claimed only as two measured
  numbers and one property (run-to-run replay and forks inside the mixed batch) from a head-to-head with a
  per-family-template control arm (§17.5, §17.14; GATE-PHYLUM-03; KILL-PHYLUM; BET-18).

**43. Requirements replace rewards** (H-13, NARROWED)
- *Strong form.* Temporal-logic requirements with a smoothed robustness score serve as the training signal with
  no loss, and cut reward exploitation to 0.1% or less.
- *Refutation.* FACT: the published successes need subgoal decomposition or added terms, and the 0.1% figure was
  unsupported and withdrawn. A minimum over predicates in mixed units is not a margin until each is normalized.
- *Survives.* Bounded-time requirements over unit-normalized predicates as the acceptance metric and the
  falsification score; training keeps reward terms (C-30; §24.5, §24.6; KILL-SPEC-REWARD; BET-24).

**44. A fixed-n rule with an unreachable verdict, and a speed test with no power**
- *Strong form.* A frozen four-verdict comparison rule at 64 pairs; a speed test read as evidence against a
  ten-fold.
- *Refutation.* FACT (executed enumeration): of the 2,145 count pairs reachable at n = 64, 521 promote, 521 are
  inferior, 1,103 are inconclusive and none is a practical tie; the first tie appears at n = 86. FACT (executed
  pilot): shared overhead bounded the speed test near 1.46, so a failure to show ten was guaranteed.
- *Survives.* A pre-analysis of which verdicts are reachable at the declared n (`eval.preanalyze`; §24.12;
  GATE-ARENA-05, GATE-ATEL-06). The ceiling 1/(1 − f + f/r + h) is recorded before any speed comparison, and a
  comparison whose ceiling is below its claim is refused (GATE-AUGUR-14).

**45. Methods considered for one decision core and rejected.** Each was weighed against a named core of §28.19 and
is not built; §28.19.4 holds the full list.

| Method | Core | Why it is not built | Used instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| Bandit allocation (upper confidence bounds, posterior sampling) | DC-02, DC-09 | Regret is the wrong objective for identifying the best design; on dispatch it is a second controller on shared telemetry | The fixed halving table, and the conformal ladder where rank agreement licenses it |
| Expected information gain | DC-05 | It buys knowledge that cannot change the choice | Value of information per cost, as a stop rule |
| Gaussian-process surrogates over designs | DC-02, DC-05 | No validated kernel exists on typed designs of varying topology | `fs-bo` on continuous sub-problems only |
| Kernel two-sample and optimal-transport drift tests | DC-07 | No power at twenty scalar residuals | A conformal band with an exchangeability alarm |
| Mechanism design for the queue | DC-09 | Estimates come from the system, not from agents | Size classes with preemption and resume |
| Replicated consensus, gossip membership, escrow counters | DC-09 | One daemon per host; nothing is replicated | A single-writer job ledger |
| A learned correction of the fast tier, or a learned step surrogate as a screen | DC-01, DC-02 | It hides the artifacts the trust ladder exists to expose | Paired tier correction on referee episodes that were run |
| Adaptive-fidelity stopping as the decision | DC-01, DC-02 | Stopping a pair early on cheap labels does not compute the sealed fixed-n decision | Kept as a scheduling heuristic that orders work |
| Coalition-value attribution as the primary output | DC-06 | Values can be negative and cannot establish necessity | Interventional contrasts, with a budgeted summary |

**46. Unconstrained zero-sum adversarial worlds**
- *Strong form.* Search or train an adversary over worlds with no constraint beyond the robot's failure.
- *Refutation.* The adversary wins with worlds no robot could solve, which says nothing about the design.
- *Survives.* Search inside a legality set, with a solvability oracle and a witness for every reported world
  (§26.3, §26.5; GATE-NEMESIS-02).

#### C.2.6 Algebra, synthesis, proofs and certificates

**47. Continuation on the GPU, with root certificates checked inside a proof kernel**
- *Strong form.* Track homotopy paths on the GPU and certify thousands of roots by α-theory inside a proof kernel
  in half a second.
- *Refutation.* Path tracking needs double precision and Metal has none. FACT (literature): the nine-point
  four-bar problem has 1,442 cognate triples, 3 × 1,442 = 4,326 solutions, found among 286,720 paths of the best
  multihomogeneous homotopy, and that one-time solve was priced at zero. Exact arithmetic on thousands of roots
  inside a proof kernel in half a second has no basis, and an α-test proves a nearby nonsingular root and nothing
  about completeness.
- *Survives.* The CPU lane [M]: double precision, a monodromy start with a trace test, and the α-test
  α(f, z) < (13 − 3√17)/4 ≈ 0.157671 evaluated by the exact rational checker outside any proof kernel, with the
  labels `Complete` and `Partial` (C-34; §25.21.4; GATE-AUGUR-22; KILL-SYNTH-CONT).

**48. Quaternionic irreducibles**
- *Strong form.* Equivariant layers must implement the real, complex and quaternionic types of irreducible
  representation.
- *Refutation.* The type is read from the Frobenius–Schur indicator ι(χ) = |Γ|⁻¹·Σ_g χ(g²), and quaternionic type
  means ι = −1. The smallest group with such a representation is the quaternion group of order 8, whose
  two-dimensional character gives (2 + 2 + 6·(−2))/8 = −1; it is not a point group. Every irreducible of C₂,
  C₂ × C₂, the dihedral groups and S₄ is of real type, and Cₙ with n ≥ 3 adds complex type only: for C₃,
  (1 + ω² + ω)/3 = 0.
- *Survives.* Real and complex blocks only (§13.5; ADR-MARROW-07). A quaternionic constituent is refused
  (`FROB-REF-214`), because meeting one means the group computation is wrong.

**49. "Reynolds averaging mishandles complex irreducibles"**
- *Strong form.* Typed irreducible blocks correct an error that Reynolds averaging makes on complex-type
  representations.
- *Refutation.* The Reynolds operator W ↦ |Γ|⁻¹·Σ_g ρ_out(g)·W·ρ_in(g)⁻¹ is the exact projector onto the
  equivariant maps for any real representation. It is more expensive, not wrong.
- *Survives.* The blocks as a parameterization, with the Reynolds operator kept as their test oracle (§13.5;
  GATE-MARROW-04; KILL-MARROW-BLOCKS).

**50. Ornamental labels**
- *Strong form.* Mechanisms named as decorated cospans, a sub-PROP, Futamura projections, Pearl-causal models,
  Wasserstein robustness, cellular sheaves or conformal geometric algebra; max-plus cycle means to balance two
  machines.
- *Refutation.* D-2 binds vocabulary: a term from a richer theory is used only where a theorem of that theory is
  used. In each case no such theorem was invoked, and the mechanism underneath is an ordinary one.
- *Survives.* Each mechanism under its plain name: composition over typed ports (C-12), build-time partial
  evaluation (C-14), interventions on a forkable simulator (C-29), a worst case over a bounded set (C-17), a
  least-squares pose graph (§20.14), primitive intersection tests (§18.3), a role table for two machines
  (§21.17).

**51. "Proved once at M1"**
- *Strong form.* The soundness theorems of the proof layer are proved once at M1 and reused. The obligations
  include identical trajectories under a quotient, equivalence of fixed-point firmware, and a quantified barrier
  condition for high-dimensional dynamics.
- *Refutation.* FACT (sibling code): the proof tool implements a decision-tree tactic and linear integer
  arithmetic; its other tactics are type stubs, and the library modules on Lie groups, matrix exponentials and
  representation theory are blocked. The listed obligations are not statements those checkers can decide, and no
  route to the theorems without tactics was given.
- *Survives.* Four evidence classes, with instance certificates checked in exact arithmetic (§12.2, §12.4).
  Reflection as an [F] pilot at M5 (GATE-LEMMA-05; KILL-REFLECT). The limits stated in §12.5. The word
  "verified" is not used for results.

**52. From mission to design by solving, at whole-robot scale** (H-17, NARROWED)
- *Strong form.* Polynomial synthesis returns all real branches of a whole-robot design problem, and three
  solvers produce candidate designs for a mission in minutes.
- *Refutation.* FACT (sibling code): the exact real solver is capped at 8 variables, 64 equations, degree 256 and
  4,096-bit coefficients, on zero-dimensional ideals only, and no sibling has a homotopy solver. The nine-point
  problem of entry 47 alone has 4,326 solutions.
- *Survives.* A bounded exact lane [F] (KILL-SYNTH-LINKAGE). Trajectory optimization with design variables as
  local refinement: about 12 degrees of freedom, up to 17 parameters, 4 to 10 minutes (KILL-SYNTH-TRAJOPT).
  Monotone co-design for actuator, battery and compute selection, with no measured timing. Continuation [M] on
  the CPU (§25.21). The pass rate of synthesized designs under independent closed-loop evaluation is unknown, so
  every synthesized candidate passes the ordinary ladder.

**53. Posynomial feasibility by integer-linear arithmetic, and sampling only inside a certified inner ellipsoid**
- *Strong form.* The substitution y = ln θ makes posynomial constraints linear, so an integer-linear decision
  procedure settles feasibility; and candidates are sampled only inside a certified inner ellipsoid, for a
  seventy-fold yield.
- *Refutation.* The logarithm of a posynomial is a log-sum-exp, which is convex and not linear, and the procedure
  is integer-linear. An inner ellipsoid cuts off the feasibility boundary, where optima sit, and misses
  disconnected components.
- *Survives.* Monomial screens and proved interval exclusion boxes; certificate-backed local boxes, with the
  unresolved remainder reported (§25.6, §25.7).

**54. Topological and exact reachability proofs over contact modes**
- *Strong form.* A Conley-index or cylindrical-decomposition argument proves reachability across all contact
  modes.
- *Refutation.* DESIGN arithmetic: k contacts with three states have 3ᵏ strata, 81 for four feet, and
  cylindrical decomposition is doubly exponential beyond four or five variables.
- *Survives.* Mode strata counted up to symmetry: (3⁴ + 3·3²)/4 = 27 for four feet under C₂ × C₂ (§13.6).
  Max-plus timing for schedules (C-10).

**55. A probability bound for runtime monitors, "valid under shift"**
- *Strong form.* Breach probability is bounded by r_breach·δ₁ + r_alarm·δ₂ + δ₃ over 14 strata, and the bound
  survives distribution shift.
- *Refutation.* The first term counts breaches twice. A monitor cannot label its own silent breaches. The strata
  number 27 (entry 54), not 14. A shift moves δ₁.
- *Survives.* A conditional bound with breach labels that are independent of the monitor, and shifted worlds
  reported separately (§22.12; GATE-SHIELD-03).

**56. A new family query as a capability, and a faster solver as the lever**
- *Strong form.* Family certificates and minimum-tag queries are a capability no existing tool has, and a
  specialized solver for them gives a ten-fold decision.
- *Refutation.* FACT (executed pilots): standard exact methods (projection, Fourier–Motzkin elimination,
  satisfiability modulo theories, belief-game elimination) returned the same complete answers on every fixture.
  A specialized solver measured 1.345 warm and 1.088 cold against a composed one, and its batch held a residual of
  575.7 µs that the solver does not touch (parsing, assembly, checking, serialization and harness, not separately
  timed), so no solver could have shown more than about 1.46 (842.8 ÷ 575.7).
- *Survives.* The query is kept where deriving the abstraction from a rich plant is cheaper than an expert doing
  it by hand. A tie with the generic comparator ships the generic method behind the same verbs (§25.22;
  KILL-AUGUR-TIE, KILL-FAMILYCERT; BET-14, BET-19).

#### C.2.7 Reality, structure and the thesis

**57. Conformal reality bands for designs that were never built** (H-14, NARROWED)
- *Strong form.* With 20 paired real episodes, split-conformal bands reach nominal coverage on held-out real
  episodes for a robot class.
- *Refutation.* Coverage is marginal and holds only inside the exchangeable set that was calibrated. DESIGN
  arithmetic: at n = 20 and 90% nominal, realized coverage is distributed as Beta(19, 2), with a standard
  deviation of 6.3 points; at n = 200 it is Beta(181, 20), with 2.1 points. Nothing follows for a design with no
  calibration data.
- *Survives.* Bands per calibrated cell, one robot and one task family, and a refusal elsewhere (§30.12;
  GATE-GROUND-06, GATE-GROUND-07; KILL-BAND; BET-28).

**58. Generative structure as a decision-time lever** (H-12, DEFERRED)
- *Strong form.* Topology optimization on telemetry load spectra yields links at least 15% lighter and counts
  toward the ten-fold.
- *Refutation.* FACT (published results): links 7 to 30% lighter than engineered baselines, which is 2 to 8% of
  robot mass on the one quadruped with a published mass breakdown, at CPU-hours for 12 to 20 links. It improves the
  built object and shortens no decision.
- *Survives.* Foundry as an output-quality subsystem, outside the thesis of §2; its beam rung also runs inside
  screening as a validity check on declared link walls (§31.2, §31.7, §31.23; KILL-TOPOPT; BET-29).

**59. Engine speed alone, and information alone** (H-18, ACTIVE)
- *Strong form.* Either a faster step delivers the ten-fold, or the engine does not matter and information is the
  whole lever.
- *Refutation.* DESIGN arithmetic on published shares: an infinitely fast step speeds training by at most
  1/(1 − s), which is 7.7 at a step share of 87% and 1.4 at 29%. With one design per agent turn and 300 s of
  training the ratio is at most (300 + t)/t, 3.5 to 6 for t between 120 and 60 s. No instrumented design session
  exists. Where every candidate is trained, training is about 96% of an illustrative campaign, so the engine must
  hold parity; once controller acquisition is amortized, agent turns are about two thirds of elapsed time. Which
  regime a mission is in is a measurement.
- *Survives.* Throughput as a parity requirement (§36.3; KILL-PARITY). The levers of §2.4. A ten-fold test that
  measures decisions, with a phase ledger (§39).

#### C.2.8 Claims, wording and process

**60. Headline multipliers**
- *Strong form.* Campaign ratios of 10, 16, 12 and 10; "90 to 99% of futile simulations deleted"; a quotient
  factor of 29.96; a lifecycle factor of 1,800; an orbit search 3,000 times faster than training.
- *Refutation.* FACT (recomputation in the planning record): the four ratios are 9.79, 6.41, 11.6 and 5 on their
  own fixtures, and the 11.6 falls to 2.0 with a mirror symmetry only. DESIGN arithmetic: 29.96 = 735,471/24,546
  is a property of one ballast fixture, and the factor is 1 in general; the 1,800 counts substeps, not wall time
  or field life; the orbit figure omitted the per-step Jacobians and was about 65 times optimistic, 37 ms against
  about 2.4 s for 256 orbits. None is a measurement.
- *Survives.* The mechanisms, each with its ceiling stated before any speed test (D-10). No multiplier is printed
  as a result.

**61. Withdrawn differentiators.** §1.3 lists sixteen statements of version 0.2 that are withdrawn. The reason in
each case is FACT: incumbent documentation at pinned versions (§1.1, §1.2).

| Withdrawn claim | What contradicts it | What is claimed instead |
|---|---|---|
| Deterministic GPU replay as such; mixed topologies, determinism and articulated contact together as an incumbent gap | Two incumbents document opt-in bitwise run-to-run determinism; one experimental solver documents all three | Replay of one world alone on the CPU from its address, bit for bit where class D1 is reached (N6); batch-composition invariance (§35.5) |
| "The CPU version is a different code base"; one kernel source for several backends, and several tiers, as novelties | One kernel compiler emits CPU and CUDA code from one source | A CPU twin per kernel with a recorded divergence ledger (§21.13) |
| Different robot topologies in one GPU batch, as a capability | One environment suite and two solvers of one engine document it | Two measured numbers from the head-to-head (N11; §17.14) |
| Electrical and thermal actuator modelling as a novelty | Incumbents ship both | Cards with provenance, and the thermal-runaway check (§15.7) |
| "No training needed" for baseline control | Published sampling controllers run on incumbent engines, with cost weights chosen per task and robot | A baseline synthesized per morphology class, conditional on BET-06 (N7) |
| Anytime-valid comparison as a novelty of the field | Published sequential tests compare policies | Those tests applied to paired design comparison on common world addresses (N4) |
| Apple-GPU support as a first; interior scenes as a novelty; an agent interface to a simulator as such | One incumbent lowers to Metal; interior generators and simulator-facing agent servers exist | A Metal tier measured against that incumbent (N10); verbs with budgets, provenance and typed refusals (N9) |
| "Morphology is fixed before the simulator starts"; an impossible inertia "loads without complaint" everywhere | State-preserving recompilation exists on the CPU; one incumbent's compiler rejects impossible inertias | The latency from an edit to the first GPU step, measured (§17.8) |
| Any ten-fold that rests on per-step throughput, on sampling control as the evaluator, or on a published ten-fold | None is supported (entries 26 and 59) | The thesis of §2 as a conjunction, tested in §39 |

**62. Corrected targets and narrative figures**
- *Strong form (version 0.2).* A quadruped training gate of 15 minutes; a humanoid throughput target presented
  as parity; 400 k env-steps/s beside a target of 2.0 M world-steps/s; an incumbent loop that teaches an agent
  its mistake "six hours later".
- *Refutation.* FACT: a quadruped joystick policy trains in about 410 s on one RTX 4090, so the 15-minute gate
  was slower than the incumbent, and incumbents publish about 2.55 M humanoid world-steps/s, about five times the
  old target. DESIGN arithmetic: 400 k env-steps/s at decimation 10 is 4.0 M world-steps/s, twice the stated
  2.0 M. Published loops take about seven minutes for a flat-ground quadruped and under 30 minutes for humanoids.
- *Survives.* GATE-CORTEX-05 at no more than 1.25 times the incumbent re-measured on the same card. Throughput
  parity at 0.67 of the incumbent or better, with KILL-PARITY below 0.33. World-steps and env-steps defined with
  decimation in §36.2.

**63. Posture and process.** Items that were proposed as ways of working and are not adopted.

| Item | Evidence against | What the plan does instead |
|---|---|---|
| Copying the foundations wholesale | FACT: the closure of the multibody crate is 42 crates and about 1.57 million lines of library source; the M1 set is 65 crates and about 2.06 million | Consume by path behind the airlock; a worker process as the first fallback; a minimal lifted referee set last (§33.2, §33.14; KILL-FSIM) |
| Numbers called verified by scripts that are not in the repository | FACT: one re-derivation gave 21.1 s where 33.5 s was printed for a thermal limit time | Known answers are derived on file in this repository before a gate pins them (§38; Appendix M) |
| Gates that need hardware a simulator lacks: a real-board timing receipt, bench validation, review panels, real building scans | A simulator cannot produce the evidence | Import lanes with a recorded `no-claim` (§30.2, §30.17) |
| Latency slogans: a zero-millisecond topology stall, sub-millisecond compiles, a preflight budget summed from invented stage budgets | None was measured | Percentiles at gates: `design check` under 2 s at the 95th percentile (GATE-ATEL-02); the edit-to-first-step latency measured (§17.8) |
| Third-party tools used without admission | They bypass the admission gates of §33.6 | Admission by an INT gate, or not used; the portable GPU layer is recorded as not admitted (§21.19) |
| A local patch or fork of a sibling in place of a change in its own repository | Every sibling is the owner's; a local patch diverges from the sibling's head and does not survive a pin move (§33.8) | Upstream work items with fixtures, done in the sibling's repository under its own rules (Appendix F) |
| Absolute statements about incumbents, and a legality bound with no cited source | Documentation at pinned versions contradicts several (§1.2) | Quoted documentation only; dimensional norms from cited sources (OPEN-HAB-1) |
| Hardware and memory figures asserted as fact: compressed buildings, resident-world counts, bandwidths, matrix rates | Unmeasured | The hardware fingerprint and world-bank gates (GATE-SPARK-07, GATE-HAB-12) |
| A score given to an idea during ideation, used as evidence | A score is not a calculation, a pilot or a measurement | Verdicts change only on evidence of those three kinds (§0.8) |
| Simulated humans as validated agents; markets over designs | Unvalidatable; no baseline exists | Scripted humans as scene dressing (§19.7; KILL-DRESSING) |
| Automated generation of bare-metal board support | Outside the safety boundary of §37.9 | Firmware export [M] compiles only against interfaces the caller supplies (§31.13) |

#### C.2.10 Four further entries

These four were proposed late and are recorded so that they are not proposed again. Entries 64 to 66 restate, from
a different starting point, mechanisms that entries 24, 22 and 54, and 9 already deflate; entry 67 is new.

**64. A module algebra interpreted at run time around the step**
- *Strong form.* Wrap the running step in a composition layer that is evaluated every step.
- *Refutation.* Composition is a compile-time fact about ports. At run time it adds an indirection to every read
  of a write-dense state, and it cannot express contact coupled through the base (entry 24).
- *Survives.* Modules compose at compile time into flat tables (§14.6, C-12).

**65. A safety certificate per contact stratum of the full robot, or one lifted-linear predictive model for the
whole hybrid system**
- *Strong form.* Certify safety stratum by stratum, or plan with one lifted linear model across every contact mode.
- *Refutation.* DESIGN arithmetic: strata grow as 3ᵏ in the number of contacts k, 81 at four and 531,441 at
  twelve. A lifted linear model has no representation of an impact jump, and its parameter count grows
  quadratically in the lifted dimension (entry 22: 1,891² ≈ 3.58 × 10⁶).
- *Survives.* The energy budget, reduced-order filters with a measured margin, and small reachable-set tables
  (§22.11, §22.12).

**66. Time-parallel rollouts, and an asynchronous event calendar as the core stepping model**
- *Strong form.* Integrate in parallel in time with coarse and fine propagators, or advance each world on its own
  event calendar.
- *Refutation.* Contact breaks the agreement between coarse and fine propagators that time-parallel schemes need.
  Policy and sensor ticks at 50 to 100 Hz bound the lookahead of any calendar in a cluttered interior to 5 to 20
  physics steps (entry 9).
- *Survives.* The lockstep batched step and the transactional referee (§16.5, §16.6).

**67. 64-bit sums accumulated with device atomics on every backend**
- *Strong form.* Accumulate counts and integer sums on the device with 64-bit atomic adds.
- *Refutation.* FACT to recheck (§21.26, re-run by the fingerprint): 64-bit integer atomic add and
  compare-and-swap are reported absent on Metal, and one backend without them forbids them in a mixed run (§21.6,
  rule 7). DESIGN arithmetic: a 32-bit count wraps after 2³² increments, which a run at 4 M world-steps/s reaches
  in 18 minutes if one bin receives every sample.
- *Survives.* 32-bit counts with a no-wrap bound checked at plan time and host drains into 64-bit totals; a
  two-limb reduction in a fixed order for wider sums (§21.26; GATE-SPARK-23).

### C.3 Discrepancies found

Every refuting calculation above was recomputed for this appendix. Six figures in the planning record or in other
chapters did not follow exactly from their own formulas, and two assignments differed between chapters. None
changes a conclusion. Items 2, 3, 4, 6 and 8 have since been corrected in the chapters, as each item now says.

1. **Refinement cost at n = 18 (entry 1).** The planning record gives 2.75 for the ratio of refinement cost to
   factorization cost at n = 18. With k = 6 steps, 9k/n = 3.0. §18.12 prints 3.0 and is correct; §21.20 omits the
   case.
2. **Step share and its ceiling (entry 59).** §2.2 prints a step share of 87%, and §2.3 printed a ceiling of
   about 7.8. 1/(1 − 0.87) = 7.69; the two agreed only at a share of 87.2%. Corrected: the ceiling is printed as
   about 7.7 (1/(1 − 0.87)), and the share is re-read from its primary source before §39 cites it.
3. **Overhead share and ceiling of the solver pilot (entries 44 and 56).** §2.3 and §2.5 printed an unremovable
   share of 94%, a measured ratio of 1.345 and a ceiling of 1.46, and 1.345/0.94 is 1.43. Corrected: the share
   attribution is withdrawn. The specialized batch holds a residual of 575.7 µs that the solver does not touch and
   that was not separately timed, and the ceiling of about 1.46 is 842.8 ÷ 575.7.
4. **Orbit cost as a share of training (entry 30).** §0.8 printed "2% to over 100% of the cost of a training run"
   for solves of 4 to 260 s. That range is against a four-minute run (1.7% to 108%). Corrected: against the 410 s
   figure used elsewhere in the plan it is about 1% to 63%.
5. **Lattice Mach number (entry 18).** The planning record lists 5.2 at 2 mm cells beside 0.35 at 10 cm. At one
   flow speed of 1 m/s the value at 2 mm is 17.3; 5.2 corresponds to 0.3 m/s. §19.10.1 prints 0.35, 3.5 and 6.9
   at one speed and is consistent.
6. **Copy cost of a fork (entry 10).** §21.20 printed "about a millisecond" and "a quarter of one batch step" for
   a copy of 0.25 GB. At the 1,008 GB/s of §36.11, read plus write is 0.49 GB of traffic, about 0.5 ms, an eighth
   of a 4.1 ms batch step. Corrected to about 0.5 ms.
7. **Milestone of lifecycle compression (C.1.4, item 12).** The milestone skeleton lands lifecycle compression
   with frontier physics at M6; §31.19 records GATE-FOUNDRY-12 and KILL-LIFECYCLE at M8. The register places both
   at M6 (§40.7), and §43.9 agrees.
8. **Tier of kernel reflection (C.1.2).** §12.4 tiers kernel reflection [F] with a pilot at M5; the foundation
   rows of §33 marked the same dependency [M]. Corrected: §33 now carries [F], as §12.4 does.

## Appendix D. Mathematics compendium

Conventions are those of §11. g = (R, p) = `a_from_b` is a rigid transform acting as x_a = R·x_b + p; ξ = (ω, v) is
a twist, angular part first, body-frame by default; w = (τ, f) is a wrench, torque first; μ = (π, ℓ) is a momentum.
The coadjoint convention is stated once and used everywhere: the algebra coadjoint is the dual map
ad*_ξ = ad_ξᵀ, with no minus sign; the group coadjoint is the inverse transpose Ad_g⁻ᵀ; and the Euler–Poincaré
equation of a left-invariant system in body velocity reads μ̇ = ad*_ξ μ + w. The map ξ ↦ −ad_ξᵀ is never written
ad* in this plan (§11.2).

Every row is a mathematical statement. Constants inside a formula are exact. A value that is a choice of this plan
is marked DESIGN, and a threshold a gate will test is marked TARGET; the worked numbers behind the rows are in
Appendix E. The column "Used in" names the construction, section or card that depends on the row, and the last
column says when the statement holds and when it is known to fail.

### D.1 Groups, frames and inertia

| Name | Statement | Used in | Assumptions and validity |
|---|---|---|---|
| Bracket on 𝔰𝔢(3) | [ξ₁, ξ₂] = (ω₁ × ω₂, ω₁ × v₂ − ω₂ × v₁) | C-01, C-07, T-11 | Angular part first |
| Adjoint | Ad_g(ω, v) = (R·ω, R·v + p × R·ω); Ad(g·h) = Ad(g)·Ad(h) | C-02, C-04, T-04 | g maps the source frame b to the target frame a |
| Group coadjoint | Wrenches move by Ad_g⁻ᵀ: (τ, f) ↦ (R·τ + p × R·f, R·f) | C-02, §11.2, T-04 | Same g; this is the wrench pushforward |
| Algebra adjoint and coadjoint | ad_ξ η = [ξ, η] = (ω × η_ω, ω × η_v + v × η_ω); ad*_ξ μ = ad_ξᵀ μ = (π × ω + ℓ × v, ℓ × ω), defined by ⟨ad*_ξ μ, η⟩ = ⟨μ, [ξ, η]⟩ | C-04, §11.2, §13.3, T-16 | The transpose, with no minus sign; −ad_ξᵀ is written out where it is needed |
| Three typed frame maps | Twist map Ad_g; wrench pushforward Ad_g⁻ᵀ; wrench pullback Ad_gᵀ, the inverse of the pushforward; ⟨Ad_g⁻ᵀ w, Ad_g ξ⟩ = ⟨w, ξ⟩ | §11.3, C-02 | A Jacobian transpose is a pullback; moving a wrench with the twist map loses the moment arm |
| Power pairing | ⟨w, ξ⟩ = τ·ω + f·v, invariant under a change of frame | C-02, §29.9, T-05 | Twist and wrench expressed in the same frame |
| Reflection parity | Under x ↦ P·x + t with det P = −1: polar vectors map by P, axial vectors by det(P)·P; twists and wrenches by T(P) = diag(det(P)·P, P) | §11.4, C-09, T-16 | T(P) preserves the pairing and the bracket because det(P)² = 1 |
| Euler–Poincaré, free body | μ̇ = ad*_ξ μ + w. With μ = (π, ℓ): π̇ = π × ω + ℓ × v + τ, ℓ̇ = ℓ × ω + f | C-04, §11.2 | Body frame, left-invariant kinetic energy. Check: a force-free body keeps R·v constant |
| Momentum map | μ = 𝕀(s)·(ξ + 𝔸(s)·ṡ) | C-04, C-08 | 𝕀 is the locked (composite rigid-body) inertia at the base |
| Reconstruction | ξ = −𝔸(s)·ṡ + 𝕀(s)⁻¹·p; at zero momentum ξ = −𝔸(s)·ṡ | C-07, C-08 | p is the group momentum; the second term is absent for principally kinematic systems and for zero-momentum free flight. A connection belongs to one contact stratum |
| Curvature of the connection | (D𝔸)ᵢⱼ = ∂ᵢ𝔸ⱼ − ∂ⱼ𝔸ᵢ − [𝔸ᵢ, 𝔸ⱼ]; a small loop of area da in the (i, j) plane gives g ≈ exp(−(D𝔸)ᵢⱼ·da) | §11.6, C-08, T-20 | Leading order in loop size; large loops keep the ordered product. A formula with +[𝔸ᵢ, 𝔸ⱼ] is wrong by twice the answer on the SO(3) fixture |
| Cayley map | cay(x) = (I − x/2)⁻¹·(I + x/2). On SO(3): R = I + 4/(4 + ‖ω‖²)·(ω̂ + ω̂²/2). On SE(3) the translation is (I − ω̂/2)⁻¹·v | C-05, T-01, T-02 | Rational; lands on the group exactly; agrees with exp to second order |
| Cayley angle | A Cayley step rotates by 2·atan(‖ω‖·h/2); the relative error is about (‖ω‖·h)²/12 | GB-01, C-05 | Use bounded by ‖ω‖·h ≤ 0.2 per substep (DESIGN), refused beyond |
| Planar Cayley | z₊ = z·((1 − a²/4) + i·a)/(1 + a²/4), a = ω·h | ADR-MARROW-03 | Unit complex z; the revolute coordinate is (c, s) plus a turn count |
| Inverse Cayley differential | (Dcay_x)⁻¹ = [[I + ½·ω̂ + ¼·ω·ωᵀ, 0], [½·v̂ + ¼·ω̂·v̂, I + ½·ω̂]] for x = (ω, v); its rotation block inverts to 2/(4 + ‖ω‖²)·(2I − ω̂) | C-36 | Left-trivialized: cay(x)⁻¹·δcay(x) = Dcay_x(δx). Using the right-trivialized form keeps momentum and destroys the energy bound |
| Pseudo-inertia | 𝖯 = [[Σ, h], [hᵀ, m]] with Σ = ∫ x·xᵀ dm = ½·tr(Ī)·I − Ī and h = m·c; consistent exactly when 𝖯 ≻ 0 | C-03, §11.5, T-19 | Ī is the rotational inertia about the frame origin |
| Shell rule | 𝖯_shell = 𝖯(Ω, ρ) − 𝖯(Ω_t, ρ) for outer region Ω and inward offset Ω_t; a thin spherical shell tends to I = (2/3)·m·r² | §11.5, §14.5 | Same frame and density; computed in exact rationals so the subtraction loses nothing |
| Mass-support bound | ‖c − o‖² + tr(I_C)/(2m) ≤ ρ² for mass inside a ball of centre o and radius ρ | §11.5, C-03, T-19 | Applied to a declared mass envelope, never to a collision proxy |
| Mobility count | M = 6·(n − 1 − j) + Σ fᵢ | C-01, A-01 | Wrong for over-constrained linkages (Sarrus: count 0, mobility 1; Bennett: count −2, mobility 1), which is why A-01 computes the lattice |
| Product of exponentials | g(θ) = exp(θ₁·Ŝ₁)·…·exp(θₙ·Ŝₙ)·g₀ | C-11 | Screws in the base frame; polynomial in (cᵢ, sᵢ) with cᵢ² + sᵢ² = 1 |
| Constant-twist chord | Δp = v·Δt·sinc(δ/2)·(cos(θ + δ/2), sin(θ + δ/2)) with δ = ω·Δt and sinc(0) = 1 | C-07 | Planar motion at constant twist; exact for straight travel. A unit-speed half turn in one second gives the chord (0, 2/π) m |

| Motor axis | L·di/dt = V − R_w·i − k·ω; J_r·ω̇ = k·i − b·ω − τ_load; locked-rotor current (V/R_w)·(1 − e^(−t/τ)) with τ = L/R_w | C-38, §15.4 | k_t = k_e = k in SI units |
| Motor envelope | i ≤ min(I_max, (V_bus − k_e·N·ω)/R); τ = η·N·k_t·i − τ_friction | §15.4, A-11 | Steady-state electrical model at level L1 |
| Voltage limit and field weakening | (λ + L_d·i_d)² + (L_q·i_q)² ≤ (V_lim/ω_e)² with V_lim = V_bus/√2; base speed V_lim/√(λ² + (L·I_max)²); top speed V_lim/(λ − L·I_max) when λ > L·I_max | §15.8 | Power-invariant dq coordinates, resistance neglected, non-salient for the closed forms; the inscribed circle is conservative by at most 1 − √3/2 of voltage |
| Thermal node | C·Ṫ_w = P_loss − G_th·(T_w − T_amb), with copper loss i²·R₀·(1 + α·(T_w − T₀)) | C-38, §15.7 | One lumped node; card values are `Estimated` unless calibrated |
| Thermal runaway condition | With s = i²·R₀·α: a stable equilibrium T_amb + P_amb/(G − s) exists exactly when s < G; runaway begins at I_crit = √(G/(R₀·α)). In general an equilibrium is stable where dP/dT < G | C-38, §15.7, A-11 | Constant current. Under torque control the loss curve is convex and the equilibrium vanishes at a tangency below the slope estimate |
| Entropy production | σ = P_loss/T_w + G_th·(T_w − T_amb)²/(T_w·T_amb) ≥ 0 whenever P_loss ≥ 0 | C-38 | A sign check on each assigned loss; it cannot fail for a correct model |
| Two-RC battery | V = OCV(z) − R₀·I − V₁ − V₂; dVₖ/dt = −Vₖ/(Rₖ·Cₖ) + I/Cₖ; dz/dt = −I/Q | §15.10 | I positive on discharge; R₀ depends on cell temperature; ageing advances between episodes only |
| Bus power ceiling | A source E with series resistance R delivers P only if P ≤ E²/(4·R), at V = [E + √(E² − 4·R·P)]/2 | §15.10 | Constant-power loads; above the ceiling there is no operating point and the step records a brownout |
| Four-quadrant transmission | Motoring: τ_joint = η_f·N·τ_motor. Back-driven: τ_motor = η_b·τ_joint/N. A screw with lead angle a and friction angle φ has η_f = tan a/tan(a + φ), η_b = tan(a − φ)/tan a, and locks when φ ≥ a | §15.9 | One multiplier in all quadrants creates energy |
| Rational gear law | From f₂ = r·f₁ and e₁ = −r·e₂ it follows that e₁·f₁ + e₂·f₂ = 0 | C-12, T-15 | Ideal transformer; a composition of power-preserving maps preserves power |
| Energy balance | Supplied = change in stored + dissipated + exported, per mechanism and per step, with the numerical residual on its own line | C-12, C-38, §29.9 | Heat held in a thermal node is stored energy, not loss |
| Cost of transport | E/(m·g·d) | §24.8 | Stated separately for mechanical and electrical energy |
| Froude number | v²/(g·L) | §24.8, C-21 | L is leg length, or ℓ₀ in canonical form |

### D.5 Symmetry, similarity and counting

| Name | Statement | Used in | Assumptions and validity |
|---|---|---|---|
| Equivariance of the dynamics | f(ρ_X(g)·x, ρ_U(g)·u) = ρ_X(g)·f(x, u) for g ∈ Γ_mech | C-09, T-10 | Exact reduction inside one world uses only Γ_obs intersected with the stabilizers of payload, boundary conditions and contact mode |
| Reynolds projection | W ↦ \|Γ\|⁻¹·Σ_g ρ_out(g)·W·ρ_in(g)⁻¹ | C-09, §13.5 | Finite group, linear representations |
| Isotypic projector | P_χ = (d_χ/\|Γ\|)·Σ_g χ(g)*·ρ(g) | C-09, §13.5 | χ irreducible of degree d_χ |
| Complex-type block | A complex-type pair of real irreducibles is parameterized by 2×2 blocks [[a, −b], [b, a]] | C-09, §13.5 | Occurs for Cₙ with n ≥ 3 and no mirror; no group in this plan has an irreducible of quaternionic type |
| H/K theorem | For a periodic orbit of an equivariant system, H/K is cyclic, with K the pointwise and H the setwise symmetries | C-10, T-25 | Used as an enumeration rule for admissible gait types, not as a list of realized gaits |
| Max-plus eigenproblem | A ⊗ x = λ ⊗ x; λ is the maximum cycle mean of A | C-10 | Irreducible A |
| Similarity rescaling | Under factors (α_M, α_L, α_T) a quantity of dimension MᵃLᵇTᶜ scales by α_Mᵃ·α_Lᵇ·α_Tᶜ; the subgroup fixing gravity has α_L = λ, α_T = √λ; canonical scales are m₀, ℓ₀ and t₀ = √(ℓ₀/g); n quantities of dimensional rank r give n − r groups | C-21, T-09, §17.11 | Exact for dimensionally homogeneous kernels; with power-of-two factors the scaled run must match in every bit away from overflow and underflow. Every group must match for similarity |
| Burnside counting | The number of distinct assignments of k types to slots X under Γ is \|Γ\|⁻¹·Σ_{g ∈ Γ} k^c(g), with c(g) the number of cycles of g on X | C-22, §13.6, T-24 | Γ is Γ_obs, never Γ_mech alone. The factor is at most \|Γ\| |
| Wreath product | K ≀ H = K^\|X\| ⋊ H, of order \|K\|^\|X\|·\|H\| | C-22, §13.6 | K preserves the module's port invariants and H the decorated ports; both are checked first |
| Kleene iteration | x₀ = ⊥, x_{k+1} = f(x_k) converges to the least fixed point | C-12, T-12, GB-03 | f monotone and continuous on a complete partial order |

### D.6 Missions, information and certificates

| Name | Statement | Used in | Assumptions and validity |
|---|---|---|---|
| Feasible set of a mission query | F = { d ∈ D : ∃π ∈ Π_Y ∀w ∈ W, T(d, π, w) ≠ ∅ ∧ ∀τ ∈ T(d, π, w), A(τ) } | C-17, §25.4 | W is nonempty and declared before any candidate is tested; executions must progress; a solver failure is `unknown` |
| Inner and outer sandwich | I ⊆ F ⊆ O. A union of proved inner cells is inner; a cell E leaves O only by a proof that E ∩ F = ∅; cells of O ∖ I are unresolved | C-17, D-13, §25.5 | Infeasible sample points do not eliminate their cell. F is not convex: the hull of two witnesses can contain an infeasible design |
| Monotonicity and polarity | F is antitone in W and monotone in Π and in A ordered by implication; F(V) ⊆ ⋂_{w ∈ V} F({w}) | C-18, §28.13 | The inclusion is strict where the controller cannot observe in time which world it is in |
| Design–world duality | For closed loops X and worlds V: V ⊆ X↑ exactly when X ⊆ V↓ | C-18, C-28, §25.9 | Fixed controllers; an antitone Galois connection |
| Docking boundary | Feasible first commands satisfy 1 − U ≤ p ≤ (U + ½)/2 for U ∈ [½, 1]; with the packet on time a robust causal policy exists exactly when ε < (U + ½)/4, equality excluded; with the packet late none exists | C-19, §20.16, T-23 | Unit mass, three unit ticks, hidden gain g ∈ {1, 2}, read error in [−ε, +ε]. A scalar model: no device timing follows from it |
| Two-world success cap | With equal priors and a reading of law P in one world and Q in the other, success is at most (1 + TV(P, Q))/2 | C-19 | A fixed experiment; stochastic packets |
| Majority of repeated reads | n independent reads, each correct with probability q, give a correct majority with probability Σ_{k > n/2} C(n, k)·qᵏ·(1 − q)ⁿ⁻ᵏ: 27/32, 459/512 and 3807/4096 for q = ¾ and n = 3, 5, 7 | C-19, §20.6 | Independence. Reads that share one fault bit stay at q |
| Observability Gramian | W = Σ_k Φ_kᵀ·H_kᵀ·R_k⁻¹·H_k·Φ_k; the index is the smallest singular value of W on the complement of the declared gauge, in whitened units | C-20, §20.11, A-17 | Local: full rank does not imply distinguishability, so a global ambiguity search runs beside it |
| Region certificate | An outward-rounded enclosure [c](B) of a constraint c(θ) ≤ 0 on a box B: feasible if its upper end is at most 0, infeasible if its lower end is above 0, otherwise bisect | C-32, §25.6 | Per constraint, on boxes of about six parameters or fewer. After k bisections in n dimensions a uniform box keeps 2^(−k/n) of each side, so joint paving of twenty dimensions is out of reach |
| Farkas witness | {x ≥ 0, M·x = r} is infeasible if some y has Mᵀy ≥ 0 and rᵀy < 0; for {x ∈ 𝒦, M·x = r} the witness has Mᵀy ∈ 𝒦* and rᵀy < 0 | C-32, T-21, §22.5 | Checked in exact rationals; only a strict conic witness is accepted, otherwise `unknown` |
| Stance family | With feet pᵢ = A·qᵢ + d, B = A⁻¹ and e = −B·d: Σ fᵢ = W, Σ qᵢ·fᵢ = B·P + e·W and 0 ≤ fᵢ ≤ cᵢ are linear in (B, e), c and f | C-32, §25.13, T-22 | Static, planar template, independently commanded forces; false for a passive suspension, which is why the authority model is a field |
| Dominance by unreachability | B dominates A on a cell if the outcome pair (A succeeds, B fails) is unreachable over an enclosure of every joint execution of the product system on shared inputs | C-40, §25.16, T-32 | Shared inputs are coupled at physical coordinates; outcomes enclosed separately admit the impossible pair |
| Smale's α-test | α(f, z) < (13 − 3√17)/4 ≈ 0.157671 proves that Newton's iteration from z converges quadratically to a root within twice the Newton step; reality needs the robust test α(f, z) < 0.03 with ‖z − Re z‖ < 1/(20·γ(f, z)) | C-34, T-34 | Evaluated in exact rationals; it proves a nearby nonsingular root and nothing about completeness |
| Three-pose dyad | The fixed pivot O is the circumcentre of the moving pivot's positions: 2·(Pᵢ − P₁)·O = ‖Pᵢ‖² − ‖P₁‖² for i = 2, 3 | C-34, §25.21 | Degenerate when the three positions are collinear |
| Minimal failing ideal | Admissible sub-worlds are the order ideals of the edit set E; the search returns a failing ideal from which no single edit can be removed | C-28, §26.7 | 1-minimal, not minimum; failure sets are not monotone; worst-case cost quadratic in \|E\| |

### D.7 Estimation, control and safety

| Name | Statement | Used in | Assumptions and validity |
|---|---|---|---|
| Invariant error | η = X̂⁻¹·X; for group-affine dynamics log η obeys a linear equation | C-13, §22.3 | Noise enters to first order; with biases and delays the filter is labelled imperfect-invariant |
| Attitude error | e_R = ½·(R_dᵀ·R − Rᵀ·R_d)^∨ | C-13 | Almost-global; excludes rotations by π from the target |
| Requirement robustness | An atom ψ(x) ≥ 0 with declared scale s has ρ = ψ/s; conjunction is min, disjunction max, negation −ρ; G_[0,T] φ at time 0 equals H_[0,T] φ at time T | C-30, §24.5 | ρ > 0 implies satisfaction, ρ < 0 violation, ρ = 0 decides nothing; an uncovered window yields `undetermined` (§24.5) |
| Energy budget | ½·m_cap·v² + ∫ max(P_elec, 0) dt + E_stored + E_gravity + E_other + E_reserve ≤ W_abs,min | C-31, §22.11, T-28 | Needs a physical guide with an absorber on every forward path; passive contact with any μ ≥ 0 only dissipates. Covers stops and guided contacts, not balance |
| Tube inclusion | R_mn((K_m ⊕ E) ∩ G_mn, D) ⊆ K_n ⊖ (−E) for every simultaneously enabled guard G_mn, for cells of measured states with true = measured + e, e ∈ E | C-31, §22.11 | Subsystems of at most four reduced states; the reduction is a monitored assumption |
| Single-row shield | u(λ) = clip(u_π + λ·R⁻¹·a, lo, hi) is piecewise linear in λ with at most 2n breakpoints; the answer is the least λ ≥ 0 with aᵀu(λ) ≥ b | C-31, §22.12 | Diagonal metric R; quantifier order ∃u ∀(w, e). Projecting and then clipping is wrong |
| Residual risk of a shield | Per episode at most E[N_breach]·δ₁ + P(N_alarm ≥ 1)·δ₂ + δ₃, with δ₂ the probability that an episode with at least one alarm ends in a violation | C-31, §22.12 | δ₁ needs independent hidden-state labels; the bound does not survive a shift that moves δ₁ |

### D.8 Statistics and decisions

| Name | Statement | Used in | Assumptions and validity |
|---|---|---|---|
| Ville's inequality | For a nonnegative supermartingale with E₀ = 1, P(sup Eₙ ≥ 1/α) ≤ α | C-15, §24.12 | Holds under optional stopping |
| Betting e-process | Eₙ = ∏ᵢ (1 + λᵢ·d̃ᵢ) with predictable bets λᵢ on bounded paired differences d̃ᵢ; for one bit per pair, ln E = (n − k)·ln((1 − p₀)/(1 − p₁)) + k·ln(p₀/p₁) on the promoting side | C-15, §16.8, §28.6, GB-06 | A supermartingale under the null that B is no better; pairs by world address; refused under an uncontrolled determinism class |
| Error budget for retries | α_i = α/[i·(i + 1)]; since 1/[i·(i + 1)] = 1/i − 1/(i + 1) the sum telescopes to α·m/(m + 1) after m requests and to α in the limit | §24.13, T-26 | The request is fixed before the draw, the clusters are fresh, and the level is spent whatever the answer |
| Race correction | An N-way race spends α/(N − 1) per comparison, so a null race keeps its best with probability at least 1 − α | §24.12, §28.8 | Union bound |
| Paired lower bound | With ℓᵢ = ln(aᵢ/bᵢ) over n ≥ 10 pairs, mean ℓ̄ and standard deviation s, the admitted ratio is exp(ℓ̄ − t·s/√n), t the 95th percentile of Student's law with n − 1 degrees of freedom | §36.6 | Arms run back to back in randomized order on the same host state |
| Control-variate estimator | θ̂ = Ȳ_hi(n) − β·(Ȳ_lo(n) − Ȳ_lo(N)); at the optimal β its variance is Var(Y_hi)/n·(1 − ρ²·(1 − n/N)) | C-25, §29.11 | The saving is at most 1/(1 − ρ²): about 2 at ρ = 0.7, 5 at ρ = 0.894, 10 at ρ = 0.949 |
| Multilevel estimator | E[Y_L] = E[Y₀] + Σ_ℓ E[Y_ℓ − Y_ℓ₋₁], N_ℓ ∝ √(V_ℓ/C_ℓ), total cost (Σ_ℓ √(V_ℓ·C_ℓ))²/ε² for variance ε² | C-25 | Coupled levels at the same world address; chaotic contact decorrelates long episodes |
